# Content Calendar

Schedules per vertical, for both fleets. The Editor-in-Chief dispatches the Radar Scout on
the news schedule and the Evergreen Scout on the evergreen schedule; a mode switched off in
the vertical's settings (`news_enabled` / `evergreen_enabled`) has no job at all —
`scripts/sync_crons.py` reconciles both fleets from `context/verticals.json`.

| Vertical | News cadence | News schedule (UTC) | Evergreen cadence | Evergreen schedule (UTC) | Mode |

Topic = the two 30-day clocks on a late container's demurrage or detention invoice: U.S. rule 46 CFR Part 541 requires the invoice within 30 calendar days of the last-accrued charge (late = the billed party is not required to pay), gives the billed party 30 days to contest it, and voids the obligation when the required information is missing — so the control tower's stored event timestamps, not its alerts, are what decide whether the bill stands. (2026-09-24 — 25,930 episodes, 9 models, 3 production harnesses; frontier models duplicate a write in 56%/74% when read-back cannot resolve the fault; the contract explains 81%; an idempotency key on every write cuts the duplicate rate 28%→4%; "agents reported success in 90% of the episodes in which they had duplicated an effect"). Winner: durable-execution-not-a-transaction (9.0, Synthesis) — Angle Type Synthesis: the orchestrator's state blind spot ⨂ the tool contract's inexpressiveness. Emergent collision: a contract at the tool boundary is useless unless the orchestrator records each task's state footprint, and a footprint is useless unless the contract declares what an effect means — one artifact with two halves, so a durable journal or an MCP gateway bought alone cannot produce transactional guarantees. Topic = the key-value cache, not the weights or the math rate, is what caps how many sessions one GPU serves at once; paging the cache and scheduling per token is the cheap capacity, another card is the expensive one. Sourced from the vertical's `primary_angles` ("inference economics", "memory bandwidth ceilings") + founder-voice §3 gpu_hardware ("Memory Bandwidth Is the Real Bottleneck"; "Inference Unit Economics — tokens generated per dollar per watt") + customer-truth §gpu_hardware Anecdote 1 (the vLLM/paged-attention bill cut). NOT a news sweep: no 30-day window, no virality Judge, no synthesize_topics seed; `gsc_analyzer.py --vertical gpu_hardware` returned 0 striking-distance queries (thin impressions on a young site — a bonus signal only, never a veto). Evergreen gate PASS — 5/5 rows VERIFIED on 5 hosts (arxiv.org, blog.vllm.ai, developer.nvidia.com, databricks.com, anyscale.com): arXiv:2309.06180 (PagedAttention paper, SOSP 2023 — "only 20.4% - 38.2% of the KV cache memory is used to store the actual token states in the existing systems"; non-contiguous per-token blocks; "2-4×" throughput vs FasterTransformer and Orca); vLLM launch post 2023-06-20 ("waste of under 4%" with PagedAttention vs the "60% – 80%" existing systems lose; up to 24× over HuggingFace Transformers; 1.7GB cache for one LLaMA-13B sequence); Nvidia inference-optimization blog (7B at 16-bit ≈ 14GB of weights before any cache; KV cache per token = 2 × num_layers × (num_heads × dim_head) × precision_in_bytes; Llama 2 7B at 4,096 tokens, batch 1 ≈ 2GB; footprint grows linearly with batch size and sequence length; decode memory-bound, prefill not); Databricks LLM inference performance engineering (7B/16-bit at 14 ms per token moves 14GB in 14ms = 1TB/sec of a 2TB/sec card → MBU 50%; "memory bandwidth the main bottleneck"; the throughput-vs-latency trade-off at larger batch sizes); Anyscale continuous batching ("23x throughput ... while reducing p50 latency"). Fact-check: 15/16 VERIFIED, 1 REMOVED (the "an FP8 KV cache halves cache memory" claim — the vLLM FP8 doc page returned 61 characters with no body, so the figure could not be confirmed; dropped rather than reworded, and the candidate topic that rested on it scored 7.5 and was not used). | 2026-10-08 | demand_sensing_advanced_sop | published to giniloh.com + Supabase (EVERGREEN track — reader/DB surface is not approval-gated) | Evergreen run (first on the evergreen track for this vertical; the news track also ran today — forecast-accuracy-is-not-shock-protection, Synthesis). Winner: safety-stock-forecast-error-not-demand-noise (8.5 — U9/D9/E7/A9). Topic = size the safety-stock buffer off the forecast's own error, not the spread of demand: the two common short-cuts fail in opposite directions — sizing off demand volatility over-buffers (26 units where the forecast error is 10), while swapping in a raw error metric with the textbook service factor under-sizes it (a planned 95% service level lands at 92.7%) — so the durable fix is to point the buffer at the forecast error and price each service-level rung as the working-capital decision it is. Sourced from the vertical's `primary_angles` #1 ("evaluating financial return of upgrading from spreadsheet forecasting to real-time ml demand sensing tools") and #2 ("forecast accuracy amortization reducing mape ... excess safety stock reduction") + the `ops_leader` persona wants ("working capital / MEIO math") + founder-voice §3 `demand_sensing_advanced_sop` ("Forecast Accuracy Amortization") + customer-truth §`demand_sensing_advanced_sop` Anecdote 1 (the 3-point MAPE cut that freed $8.5M — carried as a desk field note, not a gate row). NOT a news sweep: no 30-day window, no virality Judge, no synthesize_topics seed; `gsc_analyzer.py --vertical demand_sensing_advanced_sop --export-md` returned 0 striking-distance queries (thin impressions on a young site — a bonus signal only, never a veto). Winner: kill-switch-misses-half-your-agents (8.5, Synthesis) — **Angle Type Synthesis: the approval gate ⨂ the brake.** Emergent collision: OneTrust measures the failure of the *gate* (the approval queue is too slow, so one third of firms watch staff route around it into unapproved agents, and only 5% can say who answers for an AI system across its life) while Okta and eleven rivals ship the *brake* (containment that is instant and reversible, driven to the runtime layer, access scoped to the task instead of granted as standing privilege) — the fused thesis neither source states: agent governance has inverted from permission-first to containment-first, because an approval step that cannot keep pace with agent creation is itself what produces the unowned agents the brake has to catch. Thesis neither source states: the decision has shifted from *which hedge is cheaper* to *which side of it a team can actually lock*. | 2026-10-12 | agentic_ai | published to giniloh.com + Supabase (EVERGREEN track — reader/DB surface is not approval-gated) | Evergreen run (Mon 17:30 slot; the news track also ran today — one-skill-too-many-green-test, Synthesis). Winner: agent-timeout-false-alarm-budget (8.8 — U9/D9/E8/A9). Topic = who sets an agent tool call's timeout, and what they set it from: Amazon derives a call timeout from an accepted false-timeout rate (0.1%) and the matching latency point on the service it calls (the 99.9th percentile), while agent stacks tend to write one round number into the wrapper and leave the model blind to the clock. Sourced from the vertical's own `primary_angles` ("deterministic RPC boundaries", "distributed microservices architecture") + the `ai_architect` persona wants in `context/personas.json` ("microservice RPC boundaries") + founder-voice §2 pillar 1 (agents as distributed microservices requiring deterministic boundaries, RPCs and strict rate-limiting, not magic chat boxes) + the founder note in §3 `agentic_ai` (a deterministic verification gate before an external action). This is the angle the 2026-10-05 and 2026-10-08 evergreen runs both dropped for lack of fetchable measured primaries ("framework docs, not measured primaries with fetchable figures"). NOT a news sweep: no 30-day window, no virality Judge, no `synthesize_topics.py` seed; `gsc_analyzer.py --vertical agentic_ai` returned 0 opportunities (bonus signal only — thin impressions never veto a durable topic). Winner: 100-case-eval-925-coverage (8.8 — U9/D9/E8/A9). Topic = the case-count and interval rule behind a release gate: the score answers one of two different questions (benchmark accuracy over the fixed suite that ran, generalized accuracy over the wider pool it stands for — the second carries wider bars), and the small suite that actually runs in CI cannot carry the plain 95% interval printed beside it (at 100 questions a nominal 95% bar held the true value only 92.5% of the time; at 20 questions it left the possible range), so the gate's own arithmetic — size the suite to the smallest gap the ship-or-hold call depends on, compare paired case-level differences, cluster the error when cases arrive in related batches, and report the interval — is the durable content. Sourced from the vertical's `primary_angles` in `context/verticals.json` ("deterministic vs probabilistic QA", "synthetic dataset stress testing", "schema regression testing" — what a gate is allowed to block on) + the `evals_infra_eng` persona wants in `context/personas.json` ("deterministic vs probabilistic gates, trace flamegraphs, judge calibration, schema regression tests") + founder-voice §3 `ai_observability_qa` (the bifurcated pipeline: deterministic gates at 0% tolerance, probabilistic gating calibrated against human consensus — which only works if the probabilistic half reports an interval). NOT a news sweep: no 30-day window, no virality Judge, no synthesize_topics seed; `gsc_analyzer.py --vertical ai_observability_qa --export-md` returned **0 high-potential opportunities** (export written to `context/recon_proposals/2026-10-13_gsc_ai_observability_qa_opportunities.md`) — a bonus signal only on a young site, never a veto. Topic = the autonomy budget for a closed-loop remediator: what an automatic repair agent's own published limits say about how much authority it may have. The asymmetry is the article — one 2026 framework reports a 98.1% cut in repair time (170 minutes to 3.2 minutes per patch, 92% patch success), while a runtime control layer of the same class reports recovery rates of 10.48% to 46.67%, and across 16 matched runs an external gate rejected 383 self-generated fixes of which 211 (55%) repaired their trigger while degrading a case that previously worked. The durable rules: admission control sits outside the agent, the gate re-runs the cases that already passed (not only the broken one), and any automated change is capped at a canary fraction (5% of traffic, 20% error → 1% overall) held at least as long as the metric it is judged on. Winner: **owned-shuttles-or-rented-robots (8.9, Synthesis)** — **Angle Type Synthesis: the owned fixed grid ⨂ the rented robot.** Emergent collision: both moves answer the same question — more throughput through the same four walls — and price it on opposite sides of the balance sheet, and neither release states the consequence of the other. Owned side (Tractor Supply, 2026-09-23): more than $200 million of its own capital into one 865,000-square-foot building on a 116-acre site, 80 truck docks, a high-density automated storage grid and 125 shuttle units — the first such system in a network of eleven warehouses, so the payback is an exception it must prove ten more times, amortised against a forecast it wrote itself (123 stores in year one, room past 200). Rented side (Ultra, 2026-10-09): a $62 million raise ($50 million Series A led by Framework Ventures + a $12 million seed) to keep owning packing robots that its buyers rent — an up-front integration fee plus a monthly charge — with more than half a million orders packed, and a CEO who says demand has let him **raise prices**, on robots whose intelligence comes from Physical Intelligence, a $5.6-billion firm the renter never signs with. The fusion: renting did not remove risk from the automation decision, it re-cut it — the operator keeps the facility risk (the robot still occupies its floor) and sheds the residual-value risk, while acquiring a unit price it does not set and a software roadmap a third party controls. The owned number is fixed and its risk is the operator's own forecast; the rented number is somebody else's price list, and in the same window that price list went up. Topic = an agent's end-to-end reliability is set at the boundary between its steps, not inside the model: one wrong step makes the next roughly 11 to 14 times likelier to be wrong, the agent recovers from its first mistake in only 30.5% of runs and never detects it in 38.5%, while the boundary controls with measured effect (circuit breakers −89% cascade propagation, component isolation −64% blast radius, output gates 73% of silent degradation, three or more patterns −71% mean time to recovery) and the measured cost of removing enforcement (containment 14.4% → 3.1% against 2.2% for no defense, while the no-blocking variant burns the most tokens) make the containment set, not the model, the cheapest reliability purchase. Sourced from the vertical's `primary_angles` ("cascading error containment"; "compound reliability math 0.95^10") + founder-voice §3 `agentic_resilience_failure` ("Cascading Error Loops"; "State Recovery & Checkpointing") + the `infra_engineer` persona wants ("real numbers, caveats, reproduction details") + customer-truth §1 Anecdote 1 (the 10-step chain stalling at 59.8% end-to-end until checkpoints and idempotent retries took it to 98.4%) carried as a desk field note, not a gate row. Winner: prefill-decode-two-pool-gap (9.0 — U9/D9/E9/A9). Topic = a served token is two different machines, and the split between them is the next unit of capacity: prefill is compute-bound and decode is memory-bandwidth-bound, so colocating them makes each phase pay for the other's bottleneck, and the price of pooling them apart is the key-value (KV) cache the fabric carries between the pools. Sourced from the vertical's own `primary_angles` ("inference economics", "memory bandwidth ceilings") + the `gpu_hardware` positions in founder-voice §3 ("Memory Bandwidth Is the Real Bottleneck"; "tokens generated per dollar per watt") + the `infra_engineer` persona wants in context/personas.json ("real numbers, caveats, reproduction details") + the vertical's own 2026-10-14 signal pool (row 5, the optical KV-cache network for disaggregated serving; row 7, the parallelism-characterization paper). NOT a news sweep: no 30-day window, no virality Judge, no `synthesize_topics.py` seed; `gsc_analyzer.py --vertical gpu_hardware` returned 0 striking-distance queries (thin impressions on a young site — a bonus signal only, never a veto). Evergreen gate PASS — 7/7 rows VERIFIED on 3 hosts (github.com: DeepSeek's own production statistics for the 2025-02-27/28 window — ~73.7k tokens/s input per H800 node during prefilling against ~14.8k tokens/s output during decoding, prefill and decode already disaggregated with a different parallel layout per phase, 608B input tokens of which 342B (56.3%) hit the on-disk KV cache, an average KV length of 4,989 tokens per output token, 278 peak / 226.75 average nodes of 8 H800 GPUs at an assumed $2 per GPU-hour = $87,072 a day; arxiv.org 2401.09670v3 (DistServe): 7.4× more requests or 12.6× tighter SLO for >90% of requests, the per-card goodput experiment (1.6 rps colocated on one A100 serving a 13B model at a 90% SLO goal against 5.6 rps prefill-only and 10 rps decode-only, so 2 prefill + 1 decode cards = 10 rps, 3.3 per card, the authors' own 2.1x), and the transfer figure (a 512-token request on OPT-66B holds ~1.13GB of KV, so a 10 rps arrival rate needs 90Gbps); arxiv.org 2311.18677v1 (Splitwise): 1.4× higher throughput at 20% lower cost, or 2.35× more throughput at the same cost and power, over two phases characterized as compute-intensive prompt computation and memory-intensive token generation; arxiv.org 2610.18444: an optical circuit-switched KV-cache fabric matching 400G packet fabrics with adaptive routing at 0.55 to 0.78 times the cost of a 2:1 fabric, tolerating the 50 ms reconfiguration of a 384-port mechanical switch, in simulations on real serving traces with measured H100 prefill times; developer.nvidia.com: the vendor's own claim of up to 30x more requests served by Dynamo on GB200 NVL72 — carried as a vendor claim, labelled as such, never as the anchor). Marker rows=7 fetched=7 sources=7 hosts=3; de-dup (gate-proven) matched `2026-10-07_kv-cache-is-the-concurrency-ceiling` — the nearest prior evergreen for this vertical, which answered how many users one fixed card serves once the KV cache is paged per token, where this argues which machines the two phases run on and what the transfer between them costs; separately distinct from the news pieces `2026-10-07_gpu-hour-price-set-by-memory` (memory sets the hourly rate) and `2026-10-14_your-chip-stopped-deciding` (the scheduling and control plane above the part). | 2026-10-14 | enterprise_build_vs_buy | published to giniloh.com + Supabase (EVERGREEN track — reader/DB surface is not approval-gated) | Evergreen run (first on the evergreen track for this vertical since 2026-10-07; the news track also ran today — `flat-price-was-the-hedge`, a Synthesis). Winner: `your-cluster-hidden-clock` (8.9 — U9/D9/E9/A8). Topic = what a managed platform layer actually sells is the upgrade calendar: every self-hosted component inherits an upstream support clock (Kubernetes maintains release branches for only its three most recent minors with about a year of patches; PostgreSQL supports a major version for five years and ships minors at least quarterly; Ubuntu LTS runs five years of standard security fixes, ten under ESM and fifteen with Ubuntu Pro; Node.js LTS guarantees 30 months), and the bought side now publishes a price for taking that clock off a team's hands — $0.10 per cluster-hour at Google Cloud with $74.40 a month of free credit, against a free control plane at DigitalOcean with $40 a month for high availability. Winner: `freight-claim-dies-at-9-months` (8.9 — U9/D9/E9/A8). Topic = the claim-evidence floor for cargo loss and damage: a freight claim is a written demand for a specified or determinable amount of money, a shortage or damage note on a delivery receipt is expressly not one, and the windows are statutory floors a carrier cannot contract below — 9 months to file and 2 years to sue for motor and rail carriers, one year to sue at sea with a three-day written notice for a loss that is not apparent, plus a 30-day carrier acknowledgment and a 120-day disposition clock with status letters every 60 days. Sourced from the `ops_leader` persona wants in `context/personas.json` ("physical-to-digital friction fixes, autonomous execution guardrails"), founder-voice §3 `supply_chain` ("Agentic Logistics Automation … auto-disputing invalid carrier detention invoices") and its §2 dock-reality founder note, and the vertical's own `primary_angles` exception-handling line ("agentic exception handling and demurrage dispute pipelines") — whose money-recovery half nothing in this vertical had covered; the intel feed and GSC contributed no figure (`scripts/supply_chain_intel_client.py --search "claims" --recent 45` returned only newsroom items on IEEPA tariff refunds and a FedEx service change; `gsc_analyzer.py --vertical supply_chain --export-md` returned 0 high-potential queries on 2026-10-15 — a bonus signal only, never a veto). NOT a news sweep: no 30-day window, no virality Judge, no `synthesize_topics.py --seed`. Evergreen gate PASS — 7/7 rows VERIFIED on 3 hosts / 5 distinct URLs (ecfr.gov ×3: 49 CFR 370.3(b)–(c) — a claim must be a written communication asserting liability and making claim for a specified or determinable amount, and "Bad order reports, appraisal reports of damage, notations of shortage or damage … on freight bills, delivery receipts … shall, standing alone, not be considered by carriers as sufficient to comply with the minimum claim filing requirements"; § 370.5(a) acknowledgment within 30 days; § 370.9(a) pay, decline, or a firm compromise offer within 120 days with status letters at each succeeding 60-day period, § 370.7(b) the supporting documents, and the currency note 91 FR 7859 (Feb. 19, 2026) which struck obsolete water-carrier references and left the clocks unchanged; 49 CFR 1005.3 / 1005.5 the rail twin of both clocks, in writing or electronically; govinfo.gov ×2: 49 U.S.C. 14706(e)(1) "a period of less than 9 months for filing a claim … and … less than 2 years for bringing a civil action" for motor carriers, 49 U.S.C. 11706(e) the same floors for rail, both as of the 2023 U.S. Code edition; law.cornell.edu: 46 U.S.C. 30701 — the carrier and ship discharged "unless suit is brought within one year after delivery of the goods", with the notice in writing within three days of delivery for a non-apparent loss and the statute's own statement that missing that notice does not affect the right to sue inside the year); marker rows=7 fetched=7 sources=7 hosts=3, dedup matched `2026-10-08_supply_chain_evergreen_brief.md`, checked_at 2026-10-15T18:34:53+00:00. De-dup (gate-proven): names `2026-10-08_supply_chain_evergreen_brief.md`, whose published result is `2026-10-08_fuel-surcharge-peg-mpg-audit` (a negotiated pricing formula on a bill the shipper receives), and is separately distinct from the neighbouring control-tower evergreen `2026-10-06_demurrage-invoice-30-day-clocks` — that piece runs on 46 CFR Part 541, the ocean billing clocks that decide whether a terminal fee the shipper OWES is payable; this one runs on 49 CFR Part 370 / Part 1005 and 49 U.S.C. 14706 / 11706 / 46 U.S.C. 30701, the cargo-liability rules that decide whether money the carrier OWES is recoverable (opposite direction of money, different CFR title, different regulator), and it cites no FMC rule, no demurrage or detention figure and no tariff.
